❯ # STRICT VISUAL-ONLY REDESIGN — HHTdiary & TripLibre                          
                                                                                
  Use the installed Hallmark and design-taste-frontend skills to improve the    
  visual design of two existing websites.                                       
                                                                                
  ## Repository Scope                                                           
                                                                                
  1. `./hhtdiary.github.io/` — HHTdiary website (repository root)               
  2. `./TripLibre/docs/` — TripLibre website                                    
                                                                                
  Do not modify `./TripLibreLab/` or any other repositories.                    
                                                                                
  ## ABSOLUTE RULE: DESIGN ONLY, CONTENT MUST REMAIN IDENTICAL                  
                                                                                
  **This is strictly a visual redesign. NOT a content redesign, NOT a website   
  reconstruction, and NOT a rebranding project.**                               
                                                                                
  The following are NON-NEGOTIABLE:                                             
                                                                                
  ### 1. Preserve 100% of existing text                                         
                                                                                
  - Do not add, delete, rewrite, shorten, paraphrase, translate, or reorder any 
  text.                                                                         
  - Preserve every heading, paragraph, caption, button label, navigation label, 
  technical description, acknowledgment, and footer.                            
  - Preserve all existing content sections and their original order.            
  - Do not generate placeholder copy or new marketing language.                 
                                                                                
  ### 2. Preserve 100% of existing images and screenshots                       
                                                                                
  - **DO NOT replace, regenerate, redraw, crop, edit, recolor, or remove any    
  existing images.**                                                            
  - Preserve all original product screenshots, inserted illustrations, logos,   
  icons, diagrams, and other visual assets.                                     
  - Preserve the original image files, source paths, and references.            
  - Do not substitute stock images, AI-generated images, or Hallmark's built-in 
  photo library.                                                                
  - Do not add new images or remove existing ones.                              
  - Existing images may be repositioned or responsively resized ONLY when their 
  full content remains visible, without cropping, distortion, or loss of        
  information.                                                                  
  - Do not add CSS filters, overlays, masks, or effects that alter the          
  appearance of original screenshots.                                           
                                                                                
  ### 3. Preserve functionality and information architecture                    
                                                                                
  - Do not change navigation destinations, URLs, download links, or interactive 
  behavior.                                                                     
  - Do not add, delete, merge, split, or reorder existing sections or pages.    
  - Do not change routing, JavaScript functionality, APIs, analytics, or        
  deployment configuration.                                                     
  - Do not modify GitHub Pages settings or repository structures.               
                                                                                
  ### 4. Allowed changes — VISUAL STYLING ONLY                                  
                                                                                
  You MAY improve:                                                              
                                                                                
  - Typography and font hierarchy                                               
  - Colors and background styling                                               
  - Spacing, padding, margins, and alignment                                    
  - Borders, shadows, and corner radii                                          
  - Grid layouts and responsive styling                                         
  - Visual hierarchy and component appearance                                   
  - Navigation styling without changing its content or behavior                 
  - CSS design tokens and stylesheet organization                               
                                                                                
  Prefer changes to existing CSS and presentation-related properties. Avoid     
  rewriting HTML or JavaScript unless absolutely necessary for a visual-only    
  adjustment.                                                                   
                                                                                
  ## Design Goals                                                               
                                                                                
  - Reduce generic AI-generated UI aesthetics.                                  
  - Eliminate unnecessary gradients, excessive rounded cards, repetitive visual 
  patterns, and template-like decoration.                                       
  - Achieve professional, restrained, distinctive, human-crafted web design.    
  - Establish a coherent visual relationship between HHTdiary and TripLibre     
  without making the websites identical.                                        
  - Preserve the current branding and visual assets.                            
                                                                                
  ## Required Workflow                                                          
                                                                                
  **Phase 1 — Read-only audit**                                                 
                                                                                
  Inspect both websites using Hallmark and design-taste-frontend. Identify      
  visual design problems. Do not edit files.                                    
                                                                                
  **Phase 2 — Design proposal**                                                 
                                                                                
  Present proposed typography, color, spacing, layout, and component-style      
  changes. Explicitly identify how the existing content and images will be      
  preserved.                                                                    
                                                                                
  **STOP HERE AND WAIT FOR MY APPROVAL.**                                       
                                                                                
  **Phase 3 — Visual implementation**                                           
                                                                                
  After approval, implement styling changes only, independently in each         
  repository.                                                                   
                                                                                
  **Phase 4 — Strict verification**                                             
                                                                                
  Compare the original and redesigned versions and verify:                      
                                                                                
  - All text remains identical.                                                 
  - All sections and pages remain intact and in the same order.                 
  - All image assets are unchanged, including file hashes where applicable.     
  - All original image references remain valid.                                 
  - No images are cropped, replaced, or visually altered.                       
  - All links, navigation, and interactive functionality still work.            
  - Desktop and mobile layouts remain accessible and responsive.                
                                                                                
  If any proposed design improvement conflicts with content preservation,       
  **content preservation always takes priority**.                               
                                                                                
  Do not commit, push, or deploy any changes without explicit approval.

⏺ Phase 1 audit and Phase 2 proposal

  I haven't changed anything in either repo. All screenshots and inspector
  output are in my scratchpad folder.

  Hallmark isn't installed in this environment. Only design-taste-frontend is
  available, so everything below comes from that skill plus a direct read of the
  code. Tell me if Hallmark lives somewhere I should look.

  Phase 1: what I found

  Scope. HHTdiary has 5 real pages, all styled by assets/site.css. The
  vision.html, roadmap.html and triplibre/** files are redirect stubs, so I
  won't touch them. TripLibre has 3 separate visual systems:
  - Product home: site.css, a byte-identical copy of HHTdiary's.
  - 13 docs pages: docs.css, 40KB, with a sidebar layout.
  - story/: inline <style> plus site-chrome.css. It uses a cream ground, red
    accent and Source Serif, and looks like a different brand.

  The two sites are related only by accident (a copied file). The story page is
  a third look.

  Problems, ranked by effect (the inspector flagged 10–15 failures per page):
  1. Hero treatment. The hero is a dark band with a grid, two blurred radial
     glows, a dashed route SVG, and a gradient-filled headline word ("story." /
     "writes itself."). On TripLibre the phone screenshots also carry heavy drop
     shadows and a ring. This is the strongest "AI template" tell.
  2. Mono micro-labels everywhere. Eyebrows, the status strip, notes, captions,
     table headers and footer headings are all small uppercase letter-spaced
     JetBrains Mono, often under 13px. The eyebrows also have an orange glowing
     dot.
  3. Card overuse. Every block is a 20px-radius, 1px-outlined card, shown as
     rows of identical cards. The Explore row adds icon tiles, and cards lift
     and shadow on hover.
  4. Hairlines and pills. There are borders on the header, the sections, the
     footer and the tables. The nav is made of round pills, and the header is a
     glass blur bar. Status badges are mono pills.
  5. Weak scale and contrast. TripLibre's first screen has no dominant element
     (largest type is 4.2vw). Body text is 15.5px in the docs and has 90–95
     character lines at some widths.
  6. Inconsistent systems. TripLibre docs, home and story share no tokens. The
     story page's accent is a different red, and the docs use a 6-colour accent
     set.
  7. A behavior that hides content. On phones, the 3rd hero screenshot is
     display:none (site.css:425). It's an existing CSS choice, but it hides an
     image.

  A note on the inspector. It also fails pages for having no pictures, no video
  and no motion. I can't fix those without adding images or JavaScript, so I'm
  ignoring those rules. The same goes for "delete eyebrows" and "no index
  numbering", where I'll restyle the text and not remove it.

  Phase 2: proposed design

  Direction. Plain editorial documentation, with flat colour surfaces and no
  decoration. Both sites share one set of structural rules, and each keeps its
  own colours.

  Shared system (HHTdiary and TripLibre):
  - Typeface: Inter only, with weights and sizes doing the hierarchy. JetBrains
    Mono is kept only for <pre>/<code>.
  - Scale: display headings go bigger (about 6–7vw), tight tracking (-0.035em)
    and leading around 1.0. Body text is 17–18px at 1.6 leading, in full ink,
    with paragraphs capped at about 65 characters. Nothing meant to be read goes
    below 13px.
  - Labels: the eyebrows, strip, k labels and table headers become
    sentence-case, 14px, medium weight, in the accent or soft ink. No dot, no
    uppercase, no tracking. The text itself is unchanged.
  - Surfaces: no gradients, glows, grids, blurs or resting shadows. Sections are
    separated by a flat tone step and 120–200px of space, not 1px lines. Cards
    lose their border and radius. Radius is decided once: square (0–2px) for
    surfaces and images, fully round for buttons only.
  - Layout: one pair of equal outer margins, with two or three shared left
    edges. The card rows become composed splits (a heading column and a content
    column) and open list layouts. The 4-up Explore row becomes a plain list
    with icon tiles hidden.
  - Nav and footer: a flat bar, no blur, and a plain underline for the current
    page. Footer headings are plain sentence-case.

  HHTdiary: navy ink and a flat navy hero field, with mint as the single accent.
  No grid, glows or dashed route line. The headline is much larger. The CTA
  band becomes a flat full-width navy field.

  TripLibre: plum ink (#22193a) and coral (#b8381e) as the single accent,
  matching the app's own palette. The hero is a flat plum field. Screenshots
  lose their shadow and ring and sit on the flat field, uncropped.
  - Docs: docs.css takes the same tokens (ink, accent, type scale, radius) while
    keeping the sidebar, TOC and table layout.
  - Story: re-mapped to the TripLibre tokens in its inline <style>, losing the
    cream-and-red look. It keeps its serif because it's the author's essay
    voice.

  How content and images are preserved

  - Text: I only change CSS properties. No DOM text is edited, reordered or
    removed. The 01 · collect labels are real HTML text, so they stay and get
    restyled. The .nlist numerals are CSS-generated, so I leave them too.
  - Images: no file is touched, and no src, width or height changes. I add no
    filters, overlays or masks. I remove only the shadow and ring on the
    screenshots. All 17 image files are hashed now and re-checked after.
  - Aspect ratios: the hero image CSS uses object-fit: cover with a fixed ratio
    that currently matches the 600×1304 files. I'll switch these to
    natural-height contain so they can never crop.
  - Hidden phone screenshot: the 3rd screenshot currently disappears on phones.
    I'll replace that with a stacked or scrollable arrangement so all three
    show, which is only a CSS change.
  - Not touched: links, JS, routing, redirect stubs, CNAME, TripLibreLab and the
    other repos.

  Where I may need to edit non-CSS files

  1. Cache-busting: site.css?v=2 and docs.css?v=10 are cached by browsers.
     Bumping the version means a one-token change in the <link> tag of each HTML
     file. I'd rather do it. Otherwise returning visitors may see stale styles.
  2. Story page: its styles are inline in story/index.html. Restyling it means
     editing that <style> block, which is presentation only.

  Decisions I need from you

  1. Typeface: stay with Inter only (no HTML change), or add a display serif via
     a Google Fonts <link> edit for headlines? My recommendation is Inter only.
  2. Cache-busting: may I bump the ?v= query on the <link> tags?
  3. Story page: restyle it to the shared TripLibre system (my recommendation),
     or leave it alone because it mirrors your personal site?
  4. Mobile screenshots: are you OK with showing all three hero screenshots on
     phones instead of hiding the third?

  Approve this, or tell me what to change, and I'll start Phase 3. I won't
  commit, push or deploy.

❯ # HHTdiary & TripLibre — Visual Redesign Implementation Brief                 
                                                                                
  Thank you for completing Phase 1 (Audit) and Phase 2 (Design Proposal).       
                                                                                
  I generally approve the proposed direction, with several important            
  refinements described below.                                                  
                                                                                
  **Important update: Hallmark has now been successfully installed for Claude   
  Code.** Please locate and load Hallmark alongside the existing                
  `design-taste-frontend` skill before implementation.                          
                                                                                
  The objective is to redesign the visual appearance of two existing websites   
  while preserving **100% of their published content, screenshots, assets,      
  information architecture, and functionality**.                                
                                                                                
  This is a **VISUAL-ONLY REDESIGN**, not a content redesign, product redesign, 
  or rebranding project.                                                        
                                                                                
  ---                                                                           
                                                                                
  ## 1. Repository Scope                                                        
                                                                                
  The local project directory contains three independent Git repositories:      
                                                                                
  - `./hhtdiary.github.io/` — HHTdiary organization website, hosted directly    
  from the repository root.                                                     
  - `./TripLibre/docs/` — TripLibre product website, hosted from the `docs/`    
  directory.                                                                    
  - `./TripLibreLab/docs/` — TripLibreLab website, EXCLUDED from this task.     
                                                                                
  Only modify:                                                                  
                                                                                
  1. `./hhtdiary.github.io/`                                                    
  2. `./TripLibre/docs/`                                                        
                                                                                
  Do not modify any files inside `./TripLibreLab/`.                             
                                                                                
  Keep the Git repositories, directory structures, hosting configurations, and  
  deployment mechanisms independent and unchanged.                              
                                                                                
  ---                                                                           
                                                                                
  ## 2. Design Philosophy: Controlled Minimalism                                
                                                                                
  I want both websites to adopt a more minimalist, editorial, carefully         
  designed visual identity.                                                     
                                                                                
  The design should feel intentional, credible, sophisticated, and              
  human-crafted rather than AI-generated.                                       
                                                                                
  However, minimalism must not come at the expense of usability, visual         
  interest, or product identity.                                                
                                                                                
  ### Core Design Hierarchy                                                     
                                                                                
  **Typography > Layout > Color > Decoration**                                  
                                                                                
  Prioritize:                                                                   
                                                                                
  - Strong typographic hierarchy.                                               
  - Clean, purposeful layouts.                                                  
  - Consistent spacing and alignment.                                           
  - Restrained but distinctive colors.                                          
  - Clear visual grouping without unnecessary containers.                       
  - Professional, carefully composed product imagery.                           
  - Accessible and responsive design.                                           
                                                                                
  Avoid:                                                                        
                                                                                
  - Generic SaaS landing-page aesthetics.                                       
  - Excessive gradients, glowing backgrounds, and decorative grid patterns.     
  - Repetitive rounded cards.                                                   
  - Overuse of pills, badges, shadows, and glassmorphism.                       
  - Unnecessary eyebrow labels and decorative dots.                             
  - Repetitive section compositions.                                            
  - Visually crowded interfaces.                                                
  - Excessive whitespace that makes pages feel empty.                           
  - Decorative elements without an informational or functional purpose.         
                                                                                
  ──── (288 lines hidden) ───────────────────────────────────────────────────── 
   introduce unnecessary packages or dependencies.                              
                                                                                
  Do not alter unrelated files.                                                 
                                                                                
  ---

  What Hallmark added

  Hallmark loaded from ~/.claude/skills/hallmark with no problems. Its audit
  mostly agreed with design-taste-frontend (gradient text, mono and uppercase
  labels, glows, resting shadows, card overuse). It added a few things I acted
  on:
  - Cream ground with a coral accent: my first TripLibre palette hit this
    assistant tell. I switched to a plum-tinted off-white.
  - Missing safeguards: overflow-x: clip, reduced-motion blocks in docs.css, and
    minmax(0, 1fr) on the story page's image tracks.
  - Spacing and tokens: a 4px spacing scale and colour literals moved into :root
    tokens.

  I ignored findings that need content changes: eyebrow text, dashes and middle
  dots in copy, hero word counts, the "add pictures and motion" rules, and the
  story's italic "of one." (I'm keeping that for its editorial character).

  The new design system

  - Shared rules: Inter only, with mono kept for code. Labels are sentence-case
    with no tracking. There are no gradients, glows, grids, blur or resting
    shadows. Sections are separated by space and a flat tone step. Cards are
    open columns, and radii are small (2–4px) apart from round buttons.
  - HHTdiary: a flat navy hero set left with mint as the one accent.
  - TripLibre: a flat plum hero with a sun-yellow action and coral accent, and
    the app's palette carried into the docs and story page.
  - Mobile: all three hero screenshots stay visible as a swipeable row, and the
    docs ui-guide showcase no longer hides one.

    HTML files.

    in 15 HTML files.

❯ # HHTdiary & TripLibre — Complete Creative Redesign                           
                                                                                
  ## 1. Reset the Previous Design Direction                                     
                                                                                
  I have reviewed the results of the previous redesign and decided to           
  fundamentally change our approach.                                            
                                                                                
  **Discard the previous visual design direction. We are starting the design    
  process over.**                                                               
                                                                                
  The previous attempt focused too heavily on removing gradients, cards,        
  shadows, and other supposedly AI-generated visual patterns. Although          
  technically successful, it produced generic, overly flat, predictable         
  layouts.                                                                      
                                                                                
  The problem was not simply individual CSS properties. It was the overall      
  visual composition.                                                           
                                                                                
  This time, I want genuine creative design rather than CSS cleanup.            
                                                                                
  Use Hallmark and design-taste-frontend as creative design systems, not merely 
  as linters.                                                                   
                                                                                
  ## 2. Repository Scope                                                        
                                                                                
  There are three independent repositories:                                     
                                                                                
  - `./TripLibre/`                                                              
  - `./hhtdiary.github.io/`                                                     
  - `./TripLibreLab/`                                                           
                                                                                
  ### INCLUDED                                                                  
                                                                                
  **TripLibre website**                                                         
                                                                                
  - `./TripLibre/docs/` — Main product website and associated presentation      
  pages.                                                                        
                                                                                
  **HHTdiary website**                                                          
                                                                                
  - `./hhtdiary.github.io/` — Organization website.                             
                                                                                
  ### STRICT EXCLUSIONS                                                         
                                                                                
  **Do not modify:**                                                            
                                                                                
  - `./TripLibre/docs/docs/` — Developer documentation. This must remain        
  completely unchanged because it is synchronized with the package's developer  
  documentation.                                                                
  - Any TripLibre application source code outside the authorized website scope. 
  - `./TripLibreLab/` — Entire repository.                                      
  - Backend systems, APIs, database code, or deployment configurations.         
                                                                                
  The developer documentation exclusion applies to ALL files and subdirectories 
  within `./TripLibre/docs/docs/`, including stylesheets, assets, HTML,         
  JavaScript, and metadata.                                                     
                                                                                
  Do not change shared stylesheets in ways that alter excluded documentation    
  pages. If necessary, isolate the redesigned site's styles from the            
  documentation.                                                                
                                                                                
  ## 3. New Creative Direction                                                  
                                                                                
  The two websites should have distinct personalities while maintaining a       
  subtle relationship as part of the HHTdiary ecosystem.                        
                                                                                
  ### TripLibre — PLAYFUL                                                       
                                                                                
  TripLibre is a consumer-facing travel diary application.                      
                                                                                
  Use Hallmark's playful design capabilities.                                   
                                                                                
  The website should feel:                                                      
                                                                                
  - Playful and energetic.                                                      
  - Curious and exploratory.                                                    
  - Distinctive and memorable.                                                  
  - Visually engaging.                                                          
  - Human-crafted rather than template-generated.                               
  - Modern without looking like generic SaaS marketing.                         
                                                                                
  Creative freedom includes:                                                    
                                                                                
  - Asymmetric compositions.                                                    
  - Bold typographic contrasts.                                                 
  - Unexpected but purposeful arrangements.                                     
  - Distinctive section transitions.                                            
  - Interesting use of color and shapes.                                        
  - Dynamic layouts.                                                            
  - Thoughtful integration of authentic app screenshots.                        
  - Creative visual storytelling.                                               
                                                                                
  Use the original TripLibre color identity as a starting point, but feel free  
  to reinterpret its presentation.                                              
                                                                                
  Do not mechanically apply one Hallmark theme. Explore several possible        
  directions first.                                                             
                                                                                
  The website should communicate exploration, daily movement, travel diaries,   
  personal data ownership, and discovery.                                       
                                                                                
  It should feel like a real independent software product with personality.     
                                                                                
  ### HHTdiary — EDITORIAL                                                      
                                                                                
  HHTdiary is the parent research and technology organization.                  
                                                                                
  Adopt a sophisticated editorial design direction.                             
                                                                                
  The website should feel:                                                      
                                                                                
  - Research-oriented.                                                          
  - Intellectual and credible.                                                  
  - Contemporary.                                                               
  - Editorial and publication-inspired.                                         
  - Typographically confident.                                                  
  - Restrained but distinctive.                                                 
                                                                                
  Use:                                                                          
                                                                                
  - Strong editorial typography.                                                
  - Thoughtful asymmetry.                                                       
  - Publication-inspired grids.                                                 
  - Distinctive headings.                                                       
  - Clear hierarchy.                                                            
  - Purposeful whitespace.                                                      
  - Carefully designed navigation and section transitions.                      
                                                                                
  Avoid turning it into a generic corporate consulting website or conventional  
  SaaS landing page.                                                            
                                                                                
  Its identity should come from typography, composition, and intellectual       
  clarity rather than decorative effects.                                       
                                                                                
  ## 4. Full Freedom to Reconstruct Presentation                                
                                                                                
  Unlike the previous task, you are explicitly authorized to redesign the       
  presentation structure.                                                       
                                                                                
  You MAY:                                                                      
                                                                                
  - Rewrite and reorganize HTML markup.                                         
  - Rebuild page layouts.                                                       
  - Replace repetitive card grids with completely different compositions.       
  - Introduce new layout systems.                                               
  - Redesign navigation presentation.                                           
  - Create distinctive hero compositions.                                       
  - Recompose feature sections.                                                 
  - Change typography and visual hierarchy.                                     
  - Reorganize presentation components.                                         
  - Write new CSS and presentation-layer JavaScript where necessary.            
  - Introduce lightweight visual interactions or animations when appropriate.   
                                                                                
  You are NOT restricted to CSS-only changes.                                   
                                                                                
  You are NOT required to preserve the previous visual design system.           
                                                                                
  You are NOT required to maintain the previous two-card maximum.               
                                                                                
  **Prioritize creativity, composition, usability, and visual quality over      
  rigid styling rules.**                                                        
                                                                                
  ## 5. Non-Negotiable Content Integrity                                        
                                                                                
  Creative freedom applies to PRESENTATION, not factual content.                
                                                                                
  Preserve:                                                                     
                                                                                
  - Every existing published statement, paragraph, heading, label, and          
  acknowledgment.                                                               
  - All original product screenshots, illustrations, logos, and other image     
  assets.                                                                       
  - All original image content and file integrity.                              
  - All links and navigation destinations.                                      
  - All existing functional interactions.                                       
  - Every published page and its substantive information.                       
  - All existing technical claims and product descriptions.                     
                                                                                
  Do not invent new product capabilities, metrics, testimonials, user reviews,  
  or marketing claims.                                                          
                                                                                
  Do not replace authentic product imagery with stock images or AI-generated    
  screenshots.                                                                  
                                                                                
  You may visually reposition and resize existing images while maintaining      
  their complete, uncropped contents.                                           
                                                                                
  Existing text may be reorganized visually, but preserve its original wording, 
  meaning, and logical reading sequence.                                        
                                                                                
  If a proposed design cannot preserve content integrity, revise the design     
  rather than altering the content.                                             
                                                                                
  ## 6. Hallmark and Taste Skill — Creative Workflow                            
                                                                                
  Use BOTH installed skills.                                                    
                                                                                
  Do not treat Hallmark merely as a lint checker.                               
                                                                                
  Use its creative design capabilities, theme exploration, composition methods, 
  and visual review process.                                                    
                                                                                
  ### Phase A — Creative Exploration                                            
                                                                                
  Develop three materially different design concepts for each website:          
                                                                                
  **TripLibre**                                                                 
  - Three playful visual directions.                                            
                                                                                
  **HHTdiary**                                                                  
  - Three editorial visual directions.                                          
                                                                                
  Each concept should have a distinct composition, typography treatment, color  
  strategy, and visual personality.                                             
                                                                                
  Do not create six variations of the same conventional layout.                 
                                                                                
  ### Phase B — Visual Prototypes                                               
                                                                                
  Create rendered desktop and mobile prototypes for each concept.               
                                                                                
  Use the ACTUAL existing website copy and screenshots.                         
                                                                                
  Prototypes must be isolated from production website files.                    
                                                                                
  Present visual comparisons and explain the strengths and weaknesses of each   
  direction.                                                                    
                                                                                
  ### Phase C — Design Selection                                                
                                                                                
  **STOP after presenting the six concepts.**                                   
                                                                                
  Wait for my selection or feedback before implementing any redesign into the   
  existing repositories.                                                        
                                                                                
  ### Phase D — Implementation                                                  
                                                                                
  After approval, reconstruct the authorized website presentation according to  
  the selected concepts.                                                        
                                                                                
  Preserve content and functionality.                                           
                                                                                
  ### Phase E — Verification                                                    
                                                                                
  Verify:                                                                       
                                                                                
  - Text and content integrity.                                                 
  - Original image file hashes.                                                 
  - Navigation and interactions.                                                
  - Responsive layout.                                                          
  - Accessibility.                                                              
  - No changes to excluded documentation.                                       
  - No changes outside authorized website paths.                                
                                                                                
  ## 7. Important Design Principles                                             
                                                                                
  Do not automatically interpret the following as AI-generated design:          
                                                                                
  - Gradients.                                                                  
  - Cards.                                                                      
  - Shadows.                                                                    
  - Rounded corners.                                                            
  - Colorful backgrounds.                                                       
  - Decorative elements.                                                        
  - Centered hero layouts.                                                      
                                                                                
  These can all be excellent design choices when used intentionally.            
                                                                                
  Instead, identify and avoid:                                                  
                                                                                
  - Repetitive, predictable page compositions.                                  
  - Identical six-card feature grids.                                           
  - Mechanical spacing and alignment.                                           
  - Formulaic SaaS landing-page structures.                                     
  - Unnecessary visual repetition.                                              
  - Lack of visual hierarchy.                                                   
  - Generic illustrations and invented content.                                 
                                                                                
  **The goal is creative distinction, not the absence of decoration.**          
                                                                                
  ## 8. Preservation and Safety                                                 
                                                                                
  Before implementation:                                                        
                                                                                
  - Capture baseline screenshots.                                               
  - Record image hashes.                                                        
  - Record the original text and links.                                         
  - Document affected files.                                                    
                                                                                
  Do not modify excluded developer documentation, including through indirect    
  shared CSS changes.                                                           
                                                                                
  Do not commit, push, or deploy any changes.                                   
                                                                                
  Leave all work local for review.                                              
                                                                                
  ## Final Instruction                                                          
                                                                                
  Start with creative exploration and visual prototypes ONLY.                   
                                                                                
  Do not immediately refactor the existing CSS.                                 
                                                                                
  Do not immediately implement the redesign.                                    
                                                                                
  I want to see what Hallmark and Taste Skill can genuinely produce when given  
  creative freedom.                                                             
                                                                                
  **Show me six distinct, compelling design concepts before changing the actual 
  websites.**

  Reverted my previous round

  Last round I had changed TripLibre/docs/docs/*.html (a cache-version bump) and
  the shared docs.css, both now off-limits. I saved the old work as patches in

  How I got here

  Hallmark dealt Wayfind, Paste-up and Hum for playful, and Almanac, Console and
  Manifesto for editorial. I used those as raw material, then built six
  concepts that differ in composition, type, colour and personality. Every
  prototype renders from the real copy, links and screenshots, which I extracted
  from the original files.

  TripLibre, playful (home page)

  A
  Concept: Scrapbook. Kraft board, League Gothic poster capitals, taped paper
  prints, hard ink shadows.
  Strengths: Most distinctive and memorable. The screenshots become real
  objects.
  Weaknesses: Loudest and most nostalgic. It needs a poster-weight display font.
  ────────────────────────────────────────
  B
  Concept: Sticker Book. Full-bleed colour blocks (yellow, coral, plum, mint,
  sky, pink) in the app's own outlined, offset-shadow language.
  Strengths: Matches the app's UI, so screenshots and page feel like one thing.
  The most "consumer product" of the three.
  Weaknesses: The closest to a conventional bright consumer-app page, and the
  most saturated.
  ────────────────────────────────────────
  C
  Concept: The Day Line. A vertical journey spine in the app's mode colours,
  with
  each section a stop. It's built from the existing "01 · collect" labels.
  Strengths: The best story: a day is a route. It's calm, modern, and the least
  cartoonish.
  Weaknesses: The least exuberant. The spine only works because the copy is a
  sequence.

  HHTdiary, editorial (home page)

  A
  Concept: Quarterly. Journal masthead, large serif display, drop cap,
  asymmetric
  12-column grid, index-style product list.
  Strengths: The most "publication". Elegant and unmistakably editorial.
  Weaknesses: The safest of the three. It has no images to carry it.
  ────────────────────────────────────────
  B
  Concept: Broadside. Dark navy poster, giant condensed capitals, amber signal,
  hard panels.
  Strengths: The strongest typographic punch. Skimmable and confident.
  Weaknesses: Loud for a research body. It risks reading as campaign or
  manifesto.
  ────────────────────────────────────────
  C
  Concept: Working Paper. A sticky contents rail, margin notes, a serif body,
  and
  the status labels in the margin.
  Strengths: The most credible and scholarly. It's very readable and suits
  future
  roadmap and vision pages.
  Weaknesses: The quietest. The empty margins need real content to feel
  intentional.

  How to look at them

  - Gallery: open /private/tmp/claude-501/-Users-zhanchao-Desktop-project/4b8c25
    45-3cd7-4b61-ae0b-6a1c7b77e7c4/scratchpad/protos/gallery.html.
  - Per concept: each links to a live prototype, a desktop full-page screenshot
    and a phone full-page screenshot, all under scratchpad/protos/.

  TripLibre B, kept and refined

  I left the original B untouched and made a refined copy, tl/b2.html.
  - Highlight: "writes itself." is now in ink text. Cream on coral was too weak.
  - Noise: fewer fill colours and tilts, one shadow colour on the phone frames,
    screenshots and rows, and a calmer chip row.
  - Readability: slightly heavier body text and looser heading spacing.
  - Screenshots: all fully visible and uncropped, with no overlaps.

  The three new HHTdiary concepts

  The logo's own palette (navy, sky blue and orange) shaped these, and the
  dotted route in it fed D and F.

  - D · Studio: a design-studio proof sheet. A cool dot-grid ground holds a huge
    serif headline, and orange guides run at the font's real metrics, measured
    in the browser. There's a crop-marked logo sheet, the principles as an
    index, products as paper sheets, and one orange action.
    - Strengths: sophisticated and distinctive, with the most typographic
      confidence.
    - Risks: it leans on its signature move, and the guides need a webfont to
      land.
  - E · Wayfind: an institution as public signage. It's all mono, with 2px ink
    plates and a sticky station rail that tracks where you are on the page. The
    products are a departure board in which Available states are amber, and
    Explore is a four-step route.
    - Strengths: the clearest navigation and hierarchy, and the most unlike a
      magazine or SaaS page.
    - Risks: the mono is demanding for long text and may feel technical or
      severe.
  - F · Studio × Wayfind (Route Sheet): a new composition, not a recolor. The
    site navigation is drawn as a line diagram and an orange route runs the
    length of the page through crosshair stations. The principles are stops on a
    line, and each product is a line: solid for Available, dashed for
    Exploratory. Explore ends in four ink exit signs.
    - Strengths: organizational identity comes from structure. It has a clear
      idea (travel and mobility), and it's the most memorable.
    - Risks: the most conceptual of the three. The route line needs care on
      inner pages.

  All three keep a subtle tie to Sticker Book through ink outlines, hard
  geometric plates and a single warm accent, without its playfulness.

  Checks

  - Copy: D and F match the original home-page words exactly (465 each), and the
    refined B matches too. E adds only the station-rail links, which reuse the
    existing section labels.
  - Images and logos: the asset copies are hash-identical to baseline, and every
    image is shown whole.
  - Phones: no horizontal overflow at 1440 or 390px, and all nav links stay
    reachable. I fixed a bug where E's phone menu hid links.

  Things to know

  - Unpublished logo: D and E show hhtdiary-logo.png (house, route loop, plane).

    a small addition. F uses only the header mark.
  - Fonts: D and F use Hedvig Letters Serif, Geist and Chivo Mono, and E uses
    Chivo Mono. These would be new font dependencies in the real build.

  - TripLibre (5): docs/index.html, docs/story/index.html, docs/assets/site.css,
    docs/assets/site.js, docs/site-chrome.css.

  Verification

  - Content: I compared 24 pages before and after, rendered in a real browser.
    No words were lost. The only differences are the font stylesheet URLs and
    one decorative, aria-hidden "TripLibre" footer wordmark. All links, image
    references, alt texts and ids are preserved, and the old /vision.html and
    /roadmap.html redirects still work.
  - Images: SHA-256 for 30 image files is unchanged. All screenshots render
    whole, undistorted and unfiltered.
  - Docs isolation: TripLibre/docs/docs/ has zero modified files. The 16 files
    in that subtree plus docs.css and docs.js hash identically to the original,
    and all 28 renderings of the 14 docs pages (two widths) are pixel-identical.
  - Function:
    - Theme toggles work on both sites and persist across reload.
    - Mobile menus work on TripLibre (390 and 768px) and the Story page.
    - The HHT diagram links and the current-page marker work.
    - The skip link works, focus rings are visible, and there are no console
      errors or dead internal links.
    - There's no horizontal overflow at 1440, 768 or 390px.
  - Accessibility: one h1 per page, no heading jumps, every image has alt text
    and every control has a name. The contrast sweep passes in light and dark on
    HHT, the Story and TripLibre, except the item below.

  Fidelity

  The implementation is a close match to both selected prototypes (side-by-side
  images in the gallery). On HHT the real route is continuous through the shaded
  sections, where the prototype's broke. The one intentional difference is the
  added theme toggle.

  Remaining limitations

  1. Contrast on the TripLibre headline: the cream-on-coral "writes itself." is
     2.66:1, a bit below the 3:1 minimum for large text. It comes straight from
     the original B, which you asked me not to tone down. I used ink text for
     the Story page's matching highlight. Switching the home to ink is a
     one-line change if you want it.
  2. HHT mobile menu: the original had a menu button. The new nav is always
     visible, so I removed it. All five links remain reachable at every width.
  3. Unpublished logo: the HHT home doesn't show hhtdiary-logo.png (not on any
     published page today). I used only the header mark, as in F.
  4. New fonts: the build loads Bricolage Grotesque (TripLibre) and Hedvig
     Letters Serif, Geist and Chivo Mono (HHT) from Google Fonts, replacing
     Inter and JetBrains Mono.
  5. Not covered: the .md files in TripLibre/docs/ (not website presentation),
     and live external links (I checked internal ones only).

❯ # TripLibreLab — Creative Website Design Exploration                          
                                                                                
  The HHTdiary and TripLibre redesigns are now visually approved.               
                                                                                
  We have one remaining website: **TripLibreLab**.                              
                                                                                
  I want TripLibreLab to be the most playful, experimental, and creatively      
  distinctive website in the HHTdiary ecosystem.                                
                                                                                
  ## 1. Project Scope                                                           
                                                                                
  Repository: `./TripLibreLab/`                                                 
                                                                                
  Website: `./TripLibreLab/docs/`                                               
                                                                                
  This phase is **PROTOTYPING ONLY**.                                           
                                                                                
  Do not modify any files in TripLibreLab, TripLibre, or HHTdiary.              
                                                                                
  Preserve all existing local changes in the other two repositories.            
                                                                                
  ## 2. Design Identity                                                         
                                                                                
  The three websites have distinct personalities:                               
                                                                                
  - **HHTdiary:** Studio × Wayfind — editorial, research-oriented,              
  sophisticated.                                                                
  - **TripLibre:** Sticker Book ORIGINAL — colorful, playful, consumer-facing.  
  - **TripLibreLab:** Experimental Playground — more adventurous,               
  unconventional, exploratory.                                                  
                                                                                
  TripLibreLab should feel like a creative laboratory for experimenting with    
  travel diaries, mobility data, simulations, and alternative ways of           
  understanding everyday movement.                                              
                                                                                
  It should communicate curiosity, experimentation, discovery, and creativity.  
                                                                                
  I want it to be even more playful than TripLibre, without copying TripLibre's 
  Sticker Book design.                                                          
                                                                                
  ## 3. Creative Exploration                                                    
                                                                                
  Use both installed skills:                                                    
                                                                                
  - Hallmark                                                                    
  - design-taste-frontend                                                       
                                                                                
  Explore FOUR materially different design directions.                          
                                                                                
  ### A — Experimental Playground                                               
                                                                                
  A playful digital laboratory with unconventional compositions, colorful       
  interface elements, bold typography, and creative spatial arrangements.       
                                                                                
  ### B — Interactive Notebook                                                  
                                                                                
  An experimental research notebook reimagined as an interactive digital        
  experience.                                                                   
                                                                                
  Combine editorial typography with colorful annotations, diagrammatic          
  elements, and exploratory layouts.                                            
                                                                                
  ### C — Creative Coding Lab                                                   
                                                                                
  Draw inspiration from creative coding, generative art, spatial data           
  visualization, and computational experiments.                                 
                                                                                
  Use procedural-looking patterns, coordinate systems, distinctive typography,  
  and experimental compositions.                                                
                                                                                
  Avoid generic developer-dashboard aesthetics.                                 
                                                                                
  ### D — Hallmark Wildcard                                                     
                                                                                
  Use Hallmark's creative design workflow to propose an unexpected fourth       
  direction.                                                                    
                                                                                
  Choose a theme or create a custom visual identity that is meaningfully        
  different from A, B, and C.                                                   
                                                                                
  Prioritize originality and strong visual composition over conventional        
  website patterns.                                                             
                                                                                
  ## 4. Design Freedom                                                          
                                                                                
  You may freely experiment with:                                               
                                                                                
  - Page composition and layout.                                                
  - Typography.                                                                 
  - Colors.                                                                     
  - Background patterns.                                                        
  - Section transitions.                                                        
  - Creative visual elements.                                                   
  - Lightweight interactions and animations.                                    
  - Asymmetry and unusual arrangements.                                         
  - Playful spatial metaphors.                                                  
                                                                                
  Do not mechanically apply the same layout to every concept.                   
                                                                                
  Avoid generic SaaS landing pages, repetitive card grids, and superficial      
  decorative effects.                                                           
                                                                                
  **I want four genuinely different visual ideas, not four color variations of  
  the same template.**                                                          
                                                                                
  ## 5. Absolute Content Preservation                                           
                                                                                
  Use the existing TripLibreLab website as the source of truth.                 
                                                                                
  Preserve:                                                                     
                                                                                
  - All existing published text verbatim.                                       
  - All original headings, descriptions, and acknowledgments.                   
  - All existing product screenshots, logos, and illustrations.                 
  - All links and navigation destinations.                                      
  - All factual claims and technical descriptions.                              
  - The original logical reading sequence.                                      
                                                                                
  Do not invent new product features, screenshots, statistics, or marketing     
  claims.                                                                       
                                                                                
  Use authentic project assets.                                                 
                                                                                
  You may freely reconstruct presentation markup and visual layout within       
  isolated prototypes.                                                          
                                                                                
  ## 6. Prototype Requirements                                                  
                                                                                
  For each of the four concepts:                                                
                                                                                
  1. Build a standalone HTML/CSS prototype outside the repositories.            
  2. Use actual TripLibreLab content and assets.                                
  3. Render a desktop screenshot at 1440px.                                     
  4. Render a mobile screenshot at 390px.                                       
  5. Include the complete homepage, not only the hero.                          
  6. Check responsive behavior and readability.                                 
  7. Explain the defining visual idea and its potential weaknesses.             
                                                                                
  Create a single gallery page comparing all four concepts, including links to  
  full-page desktop and mobile previews.                                        
                                                                                
  Save all prototype files and screenshots in the existing scratchpad or a      
  separate temporary directory.                                                 
                                                                                
  ## 7. Selection Process                                                       
                                                                                
  **STOP after presenting the four prototypes.**                                
                                                                                
  Do not select a winner on my behalf.                                          
                                                                                
  Do not implement anything into the existing website.                          
                                                                                
  Do not modify, commit, push, or deploy any repository.                        
                                                                                
  I will visually compare the four designs and select the final direction.      
                                                                                
  ## Final Instruction                                                          
                                                                                
  TripLibreLab should be the most creatively adventurous member of the HHTdiary 
  ecosystem.                                                                    
                                                                                
  Use the freedom of an experimental product to create something memorable,     
  surprising, playful, and thoughtfully designed.                               
                                                                                
  **Generate four complete visual prototypes and a comparison gallery, then     
  wait for my selection.**

  The four ideas

  Each concept uses the logo's own indigo, cyan and violet as a starting point.

  A · Experimental Playground
  Defining idea: The page is a toy. A dark indigo play-space with candy-coloured

  pills, blob-shaped steps and bouncing capsule "doors". The logo's route is a
  live path with a bead riding it.
  Main weakness: Loudest of the four, and the closest cousin of TripLibre's
  sticker energy. The constant motion tires on long reads.
  ────────────────────────────────────────
  B · Interactive Notebook
  Defining idea: A research notebook on an indigo desk. Graph-paper pages with
  binder holes, handwritten annotations, circled words, taped notes, and lines
  that draw themselves on scroll.
  Main weakness: Reads the least like software. The handwriting font needs care
  at small sizes.
  ────────────────────────────────────────
  C · Creative Coding Lab
  Defining idea: Generative art is the page. The hero is a live flow field of
  routes with a lime play head and particles that bend around your pointer.
  Rulers and a crosshair frame the viewport, and each section has its own
  procedural artwork.
  Main weakness: The darkest and most technical. The canvas art can be heavy on
  low-end phones and is static under reduced motion.
  ────────────────────────────────────────
  D · Pocket Instrument
  Defining idea: Hallmark's Bloom retuned to indigo. A day is lanes of clips on
  a
  piano-roll grid with a pausable playhead, and the primary control is a rubber
   play cap.
  Main weakness: The calmest and closest to conventional product design. It
  needs
  two Fontshare fonts.

  Hallmark dealt Console, Piste, Bloom, Hedgerow and Furrow for this brief.
  Bloom became D. The other concepts come from design-taste-frontend's
  directions (loud poster, the page as an object, one motif) and from the
  brief's own A–C directions.

  The four-kinds-of-movement table carries its meaning visually. Observed,
  replayed, synthetic and advisory each get their own line style (A, C), ledger
  row (B) or lane (D).

  Checks

  - Copy: words compared against the original homepage plus the header and
    footer text. B and D match exactly. A and C have one extra "TripLibreLab"
    word, a decorative aria-hidden footer wordmark. No existing text is missing.
  - Assets: the only authentic asset is the SVG logo. I copied it unchanged
    (identical hash), so there are no invented screenshots. The site itself says
    there are none yet.
  - Rendering: all four render at 1440, 768 and 390px with no horizontal
    overflow, console errors or broken fonts.
  - Fix: on the phone, the header nav collided with the logo on every concept,
    so I wrapped it onto its own row with all five links kept.
  - Not covered: the theme toggle and skip link aren't in the prototypes, and I
    didn't audit contrast.

  Which concept do you want, or a mix? For example, C's generative hero with D's
  calm product sections. Once you choose, I'd extend it to the Roadmap and docs

❯ TripLibreLab — Refine Concept B into an Experimental Notebook                 
                                                                                
  I like Concept B — Interactive Notebook, but I have one important concern.    
                                                                                
  The current design is excellent for TripLibreLab's initial diary replay and   
  sharing functionality. However, it does not sufficiently communicate the      
  long-term vision of TripLibreLab as a platform for experimentation,           
  reconstruction, simulation, and advisory exploration.                         
                                                                                
  I want to prepare the visual identity for that future.                        
                                                                                
  Creative Direction: Interactive Experiment Notebook                           
                                                                                
  Keep Concept B as the foundation, but integrate selected visual ideas from    
  Concept C — Creative Coding Lab.                                              
                                                                                
  Preserve:                                                                     
  - Graph paper, binder holes, and notebook surfaces.                           
  - Handwritten annotations and colorful highlights.                            
  - Deep-indigo identity.                                                       
  - Playful, exploratory personality.                                           
  - Existing content and product identity.                                      
                                                                                
  Introduce:                                                                    
  - Experimental workbench aesthetics.                                          
  - Computational diagrams and trajectory visualizations.                       
  - Nodes, paths, branches, and alternative scenarios.                          
  - Simulation-inspired visual compositions.                                    
  - A stronger sense of experimentation and discovery.                          
  - Visual components that could support future interactive research tools.     
                                                                                
  The website should feel like a notebook where experiments can actually        
  happen, rather than merely a notebook containing travel observations.         
                                                                                
  Important Constraints                                                         
                                                                                
  - Do not turn it into a generic analytics dashboard or developer console.     
  - Do not copy Concept C's dark generative-art background wholesale.           
  - Keep the welcoming, playful character of Concept B.                         
  - Do not overload the page with decorative scientific imagery.                
  - Preserve all original published text, links, branding, and assets.          
  - Do not fabricate existing features or claim that simulation or              
  reconstruction functionality is already available.                            
  - Future-facing design elements must not misrepresent the product's current
